It started after that fight with my in-laws over the Día de los Muertos altar, and since then it’s just felt like the whole thing has been building.\n\nDoctor: What makes you so sure the parish committee is involved?\n\nPatient: Because it’s not just my in-laws saying things to me at home. It feels like they’ve brought it into the church, through the altar and the parish committee, so people see me differently. Like they’re using that to push me aside and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Yes… on several nights I heard my mother’s voice. She’s passed, but I heard her telling me where to put the marigolds and the candles. And once I saw her standing near the kitchen doorway, but my husband said no one was there.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually high or full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, there were days I felt really bright, like full of energy and sure of myself. But if anyone questioned me about the altar, I’d get angry fast… like they were insulting my whole family, not just asking a question.\n\nDoctor: When you heard your mother’s voice, were you feeling unusually high or full of energy at that time?\n\nPatient: Yes. It was during that time when I was hardly sleeping and just rushing around, feeling like I had this special duty to fix the altar. That’s when I heard her and saw her.\n\nWhen my mood went down later, I still felt very guilty and sad, but I wasn’t hearing her voice or seeing her anymore.\n\nDoctor: After your mood went back down, did you still believe that your in-laws and the parish committee were trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: After my mood went back down, I still felt really guilty and sad. But I wasn’t hearing my mother or seeing her anymore. That only happened when I was barely sleeping and rushing around, feeling like I had that special duty with the altar.\n\nDoctor: After your mood went back to normal, did you still believe your in-laws and the parish committee were trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even after I wasn’t so worked up, I still felt like they weren’t just arguing with me. I believed my in-laws were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out… to make people forget everything I’d done for the family.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you noticed yourself becoming emotionally flat or less interested in being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve become flat exactly. I have been keeping more to myself at times, but I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief that your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to push you out lasted for more than three months?\n\nPatient: No, not more than three months. This whole thing has been going on about two months, since the fight with my in-laws over the Día de los Muertos altar.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your in-laws or the parish committee have any special powers, or are they affecting you only through ordinary people and church politics?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s special powers or anything like that. It’s more that they’re using people… the altar, the parish committee, the church politics. I came in because I knew they weren’t just arguing with me. They were trying to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust tells you there is no real evidence that the parish committee is trying to push you out, are you able to consider that they might be mistaken, or are you completely certain it is true?\n\nPatient: I can listen to them, but honestly, I still feel like they’re wrong. To me it’s clear my in-laws weren’t just arguing — they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have other people told you that your speech was hard to follow or that you were jumping rapidly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Yes. My daughter told me I was talking so fast she couldn’t even get a word in. I was going on about plans and family history, and I felt like I had to keep explaining everything before the ideas slipped away.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you lost motivation to do your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed that I lost motivation exactly. And once I saw her standing near the kitchen doorway, but my husband said no one was there.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt unusually high or full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, there were days I felt really bright, like full of energy and sure of myself. But if anyone questioned me about the altar, I’d get angry fast… like they were insulting my whole family, not just asking a question.\n\nDoctor: When you heard your mother’s voice, were you feeling unusually high or full of energy at that time?\n\nPatient: Yes. It was during that time when I was hardly sleeping and just rushing around, feeling like I had this special duty to fix the altar. That’s when I heard her and saw her.\n\nWhen my mood went down later, I still felt very guilty and sad, but I wasn’t hearing her voice or seeing her anymore.\n\nDoctor: After your mood went back down, did you still believe that your in-laws and the parish committee were trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: After my mood went back down, I still felt really guilty and sad. But I wasn’t hearing my mother or seeing her anymore. That only happened when I was barely sleeping and rushing around, feeling like I had that special duty with the altar.\n\nDoctor: After your mood went back to normal, did you still believe your in-laws and the parish committee were trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even after I wasn’t so worked up, I still felt like they weren’t just arguing with me. I believed my in-laws were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out… to make people forget everything I’d done for the family.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you noticed yourself becoming emotionally flat or less interested in being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve become flat exactly. I have been keeping more to myself at times, but I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief that your in-laws and the parish committee are trying to push you out lasted for more than three months?\n\nPatient: No, not more than three months. This whole thing has been going on about two months, since the fight with my in-laws over the Día de los Muertos altar.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your in-laws or the parish committee have any special powers, or are they affecting you only through ordinary people and church politics?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s special powers or anything like that. It’s more that they’re using people… the altar, the parish committee, the church politics. I came in because I knew they weren’t just arguing with me. They were trying to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust tells you there is no real evidence that the parish committee is trying to push you out, are you able to consider that they might be mistaken, or are you completely certain it is true?\n\nPatient: I can listen to them, but honestly, I still feel like they’re wrong. To me it’s clear my in-laws weren’t just arguing — they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have other people told you that your speech was hard to follow or that you were jumping rapidly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Yes. My daughter told me I was talking so fast she couldn’t even get a word in. I was going on about plans and family history, and I felt like I had to keep explaining everything before the ideas slipped away.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you lost motivation to do your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed that I lost motivation exactly. I still feel like I’ve been trying to keep up with things, but it’s been hard to tell what’s motivation and what’s just all the stress.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, has there been even a few days when the suspicious belief about your in-laws and the parish committee went away completely?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it ever went away completely. I might get distracted for a bit, but then it comes right back — that they’re using the parish committee and even the altar to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that outside forces were controlling your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say controlling my thoughts or my actions exactly. What I felt was that my in-laws were using the parish committee and even the altar to push me out, to make people forget everything I’ve done for this family. It felt bigger than just an argument.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

They were trying to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust tells you there is no real evidence that the parish committee is trying to push you out, are you able to consider that they might be mistaken, or are you completely certain it is true?\n\nPatient: I can listen to them, but honestly, I still feel like they’re wrong. To me it’s clear my in-laws weren’t just arguing — they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have other people told you that your speech was hard to follow or that you were jumping rapidly from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Yes. My daughter told me I was talking so fast she couldn’t even get a word in. I was going on about plans and family history, and I felt like I had to keep explaining everything before the ideas slipped away.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you lost motivation to do your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed that I lost motivation exactly. I still feel like I’ve been trying to keep up with things, but it’s been hard to tell what’s motivation and what’s just all the stress.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, has there been even a few days when the suspicious belief about your in-laws and the parish committee went away completely?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it ever went away completely. I might get distracted for a bit, but then it comes right back — that they’re using the parish committee and even the altar to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that outside forces were controlling your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say controlling my thoughts or my actions exactly. What I felt was that my in-laws were using the parish committee and even the altar to push me out, to make people forget everything I’ve done for this family. It felt bigger than just an argument.\n\nDoctor: At any time when your mood felt normal, did you hear or see things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: No, not when my mood felt normal. The voices, and seeing my mother, that was only during that time when I was hardly sleeping and rushing around, feeling like I had this special duty to fix the altar. Later, when I was more sad and guilty, I wasn’t hearing or seeing those things anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I was going on about plans and family history, and I felt like I had to keep explaining everything before the ideas slipped away.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you lost motivation to do your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed that I lost motivation exactly. I still feel like I’ve been trying to keep up with things, but it’s been hard to tell what’s motivation and what’s just all the stress.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, has there been even a few days when the suspicious belief about your in-laws and the parish committee went away completely?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it ever went away completely. I might get distracted for a bit, but then it comes right back — that they’re using the parish committee and even the altar to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that outside forces were controlling your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say controlling my thoughts or my actions exactly. What I felt was that my in-laws were using the parish committee and even the altar to push me out, to make people forget everything I’ve done for this family. It felt bigger than just an argument.\n\nDoctor: At any time when your mood felt normal, did you hear or see things that other people could not?\n\nPatient: No, not when my mood felt normal. The voices, and seeing my mother, that was only during that time when I was hardly sleeping and rushing around, feeling like I had this special duty to fix the altar. Later, when I was more sad and guilty, I wasn’t hearing or seeing those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you still believed your in-laws and the parish committee were trying to push you out, while your mood felt neither unusually high nor especially depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… I believed it for a while. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me — they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you done things that other people said were very strange or disorganized?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I was acting strange on purpose or anything. People may have thought I was worked up or not making sense, but I don’t know about “disorganized.” What do you mean by that?"
